Well, I guess... um, as a kid, I wasn't really losing things or forgetting things too much. I was actually pretty good at keeping track of my stuff! But, well... I was definitely a little bit, um, energetic?

Um, well... I really try not to be rude, I promise! I don't want to be someone who interrupts people. But... I guess sometimes in group critiques or, like, class discussions, I might accidentally jump in a little early? 

It's just that I get so excited, and I think I know what the other person is going to say, so I just sort of... blurt out my answer before they're totally finished talking. And then I always feel really embarrassed right after, because I realize I just guessed what they were trying to say and cut them off. I always apologize right away, though! I really don't mean to be disrespectful. Is it okay that I just get a little too eager sometimes? I'm trying to be better about waiting my turn, I really am.

Um, well, I really do try my best to pay attention, especially for my business assignments. I know they're super important. But, um... I guess if it's a really long lecture or a video that isn't super exciting, it can be a little bit tough? 

Like, I'll start out listening really closely, but then after a few minutes, my brain just sort of... wanders off? I'll start thinking about, like, new charm designs I want to make, or ideas for packaging orders, which is still related to my work, you know? But then I'll suddenly realize I haven't been listening and I missed half of what the video said. 

I always rewind it and try again, though! I really want to do a good job. I hope it doesn't make me sound like a bad student just because my mind drifts to my creative projects sometimes. That's pretty normal, right?

Um, well, I definitely try to relax when I'm at home! I love just hanging out. But... I guess sometimes it's a little tricky for me to just stay completely still?

I promise I don't let my customers down. Is it normal that I can handle the shop okay, but the schoolwork is just... harder for me to keep straight? I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your schoolwork, do you find that you have similar trouble staying focused or keeping track of your responsibilities in your social life or at home?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really! I try really hard to be a good friend and roommate. I mean, my side of the room might get a little bit messy sometimes, but I always make sure to clean it up before it bothers anyone! And I love hanging out with my friends. Sometimes I might talk a little fast or get super excited about what we're doing, but I don't forget when we're supposed to meet up or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep my personal life nice and tidy. I just want everyone to be happy, really. I guess sometimes in group critiques or, like, class discussions, I might accidentally jump in a little early? \n\nIt's just that I get so excited, and I think I know what the other person is going to say, so I just sort of... blurt out my answer before they're totally finished talking. And then I always feel really embarrassed right after, because I realize I just guessed what they were trying to say and cut them off. I always apologize right away, though! I really don't mean to be disrespectful. Is it okay that I just get a little too eager sometimes? I'm trying to be better about waiting my turn, I really am.\n\nDoctor: When you are faced with a task that you find boring or uninteresting, do you find it difficult to stay focused and complete it without getting distracted by other things?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I really do try my best to pay attention, especially for my business assignments. Oh, um... no, I don't really think so? I always try to be on my very best behavior when I'm visiting my family or going to, like, formal things. I'd never want to be disrespectful to my relatives by spacing out while they're talking to me or bouncing around the room!

I think I do a pretty good job of just sitting nicely and paying attention when I'm in those kinds of places. It's really mostly just with my school videos or when I'm relaxing on the couch at home, like I mentioned. 

That's a good thing, right? Like, it shows I can hold it together and be polite when it really counts?

I know they're super important. But, um... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, with my friends? Oh, no, I don't think so. I really value my friends, so I always make sure I'm being a good listener when we hang out. 

I mean, I guess sometimes we get really excited talking about things, and maybe I'll talk a little fast or we'll jump from topic to topic, but that's just because we're having fun, you know? It's not like I'm zoning out on them or anything like that. I would feel terrible if I made them feel like I wasn't paying attention to what they were saying. 

So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'm totally focused when I'm with them. That's pretty normal for friends to just be energetic together, right? I'm sure it doesn't mean anything bad.

I just really want to do a good job with everything.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at your childhood or early teen years, before you started this program, did you frequently have trouble with things like losing your belongings, forgetting appointments, or being easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... um, as a kid, I wasn't really losing things or forgetting things too much. I was actually pretty good at keeping track of my stuff! But, well... I was definitely a little bit, um, energetic? \n\nMy mom actually still has these old notes from my elementary school teachers. They always said I was a really enthusiastic student! But... they also mentioned I had a hard time staying in my seat, and I would sometimes, you know, talk before it was my turn, or kind of rush through group activities. I really did understand the work, I just... I guess I was just eager to get to the next thing? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad kid. I really tried to be good! Is it normal for kids to just have a little too much energy like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have these same struggles with focus and organization when you are working in your shop, or is this difficulty primarily limited to when you are studying for your classes?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think so? I haven't really noticed those same problems when I'm working on my shop. I guess because I'm making things with my hands and moving around, it feels a lot easier to just... get it done, you know? I don't really have to sit still and focus the same way I do for my classes. \n\nMy work table might get a tiny bit cluttered with materials sometimes, but I always get my orders shipped out on time! I promise I don't let my customers down. Is it normal that I can handle the shop okay, but the schoolwork is just... harder for me to keep straight? I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your schoolwork, do you find that you have similar trouble staying focused or keeping track of your responsibilities in your social life or at home?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really! I try really hard to be a good friend and roommate. I mean, my side of the room might get a little bit messy sometimes, but I always make sure to clean it up before it bothers anyone! And I love hanging out with my friends. Sometimes I might talk a little fast or get super excited about what we're doing, but I don't forget when we're supposed to meet up or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep my personal life nice and tidy. I hope it doesn't seem like my whole life is a mess just because school is a bit tricky right now. Is it okay that I'm doing alright with my friends and at home?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior, such as acting out, getting into conflicts, or perhaps withdrawing from your usual social activities, since you started this new program?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not! Um, I really try my best to get along with everybody. I haven't been arguing with anyone or, you know, acting out at all. I promise I'm not a troublemaker! \n\nAnd I'm definitely still seeing my friends and doing all my normal stuff, so I haven't pulled away or hidden in my room or anything like that. I really try to stay positive and keep things peaceful with the people around me. \n\nUm, I hope I don't seem like the kind of person who would cause conflicts? I just want everyone to be happy, really. I'm doing okay with all that, right?\n\nDoctor: Looking at your current life outside of your shop, do you still find that you frequently talk out of turn, interrupt others, or feel like you have to rush through activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try not to be rude, I promise! I don't want to be someone who interrupts people. But... I guess sometimes in group critiques or, like, class discussions, I might accidentally jump in a little early? \n\nIt's just that I get so excited, and I think I know what the other person is going to say, so I just sort of... blurt out my answer before they're totally finished talking. And then I always feel really embarrassed right after, because I realize I just guessed what they were trying to say and cut them off. I always apologize right away, though! I really don't mean to be disrespectful. Is it okay that I just get a little too eager sometimes? I'm trying to be better about waiting my turn, I really am.\n\nDoctor: When you are faced with a task that you find boring or uninteresting, do you find it difficult to stay focused and complete it without getting distracted by other things?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I really do try my best to pay attention, especially for my business assignments. I know they're super important. But, um... I guess if it's a really long lecture or a video that isn't super exciting, it can be a little bit tough? \n\nLike, I'll start out listening really closely, but then after a few minutes, my brain just sort of... wanders off? I'll start thinking about, like, new charm designs I want to make, or ideas for packaging orders, which is still related to my work, you know? But then I'll suddenly realize I haven't been listening and I missed half of what the video said. \n\nI always rewind it and try again, though! I really want to do a good job. I hope it doesn't make me sound like a bad student just because my mind drifts to my creative projects sometimes. That's pretty normal, right?\n\nDoctor: Outside of your school environment, do you feel like you have a hard time staying seated or that you feel a constant inner sense of restlessness, even when you are just relaxing at home?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I definitely try to relax when I'm at home! I love just hanging out. But... I guess sometimes it's a little tricky for me to just stay completely still? \n\nLike, if I'm supposed to be sitting and listening to someone, or just watching a movie, I kind of get this... buzzing feeling inside? It's not bad, I promise! It's just like my body feels like it has all this extra energy and I need to be doing something or moving a little bit, even if I'm not actually going anywhere. \n\nI really try not to bother anyone with it, though! I'll just, you know, wiggle my toes or shift around on the couch so I'm not being disruptive. I hope that doesn't sound too weird? I think a lot of people probably get a little fidgety when they're trying to sit quietly, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Aside from your schoolwork, do you find that this 'buzzing' feeling or the tendency to lose focus happens in other settings, like when you are visiting family or in other structured environments outside of school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't really think so? I'd never want to be disrespectful to my relatives by spacing out while they're talking to me or bouncing around the room! \n\nSo, no, I haven't really noticed that buzzing feeling or losing my focus in those situations. It's really mostly just with my school videos or when I'm relaxing on the couch at home, like I mentioned. \n\nThat's a good thing, right? Like, it shows I can hold it together and be polite when it really counts?\n\nDoctor: When you are with your close friends, do you ever feel that same 'buzzing' energy or find yourself losing focus during your conversations with them?\n\nPatient: Um, with my friends? Um, well... waiting in lines is definitely not my favorite thing. I mean, nobody likes waiting, right? 

But, um, I guess I do get a little... fidgety? Like, if I'm standing in line at the financial aid office, or waiting to ask a professor a question after class, I just... I kind of shift my weight a lot, or bounce on my heels. I try really hard not to be annoying about it! I know everyone else is waiting too. 

And, um, sometimes in my art critiques, when we have to go around the room and wait our turn to speak... I get so eager to share my thoughts that I might, um, accidentally jump in a little early? Like, I'll cut in before someone is totally finished. I always apologize right away, though! I never mean to be rude or talk over anyone. It's just that I get so excited about the discussion, you know? 

That's just a sign that I'm really passionate and engaged with the class, right? I'm sure teachers appreciate students who are enthusiastic, even if they're a tiny bit impatient sometimes.

I don't really have to sit still and focus the same way I do for my classes. \n\nMy work table might get a tiny bit cluttered with materials sometimes, but I always get my orders shipped out on time! I promise I don't let my customers down. Is it normal that I can handle the shop okay, but the schoolwork is just... harder for me to keep straight? I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your schoolwork, do you find that you have similar trouble staying focused or keeping track of your responsibilities in your social life or at home?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really! I try really hard to be a good friend and roommate. I mean, my side of the room might get a little bit messy sometimes, but I always make sure to clean it up before it bothers anyone! And I love hanging out with my friends. Sometimes I might talk a little fast or get super excited about what we're doing, but I don't forget when we're supposed to meet up or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep my personal life nice and tidy. I hope it doesn't seem like my whole life is a mess just because school is a bit tricky right now. Is it okay that I'm doing alright with my friends and at home?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior, such as acting out, getting into conflicts, or perhaps withdrawing from your usual social activities, since you started this new program?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not! Um, I really try my best to get along with everybody. I haven't been arguing with anyone or, you know, acting out at all. I promise I'm not a troublemaker! \n\nAnd I'm definitely still seeing my friends and doing all my normal stuff, so I haven't pulled away or hidden in my room or anything like that. I really try to stay positive and keep things peaceful with the people around me. \n\nUm, I hope I don't seem like the kind of person who would cause conflicts? I just want everyone to be happy, really. I'm doing okay with all that, right?\n\nDoctor: Looking at your current life outside of your shop, do you still find that you frequently talk out of turn, interrupt others, or feel like you have to rush through activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess sometimes in group critiques or, like, class discussions, I might accidentally jump in a little early? \n\nIt's just that I get so excited, and I think I know what the other person is going to say, so I just sort of... blurt out my answer before they're totally finished talking. And then I always feel really embarrassed right after, because I realize I just guessed what they were trying to say and cut them off. I always apologize right away, though! I really don't mean to be disrespectful. Is it okay that I just get a little too eager sometimes? I'm trying to be better about waiting my turn, I really am.\n\nDoctor: When you are faced with a task that you find boring or uninteresting, do you find it difficult to stay focused and complete it without getting distracted by other things?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I really do try my best to pay attention, especially for my business assignments. I know they're super important. But, um... Um... working on my shop? Oh, I love my shop! That's when I'm happiest, really. I make all these little custom resin charms and keychains, and it's just so much fun.
